      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;HMS &lt;/strong&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;em&gt;Wager&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/strong&gt; was a square-rigged sixth-rate &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Navy" target="_blank"&gt;Royal Navy&lt;/a&gt; ship of 28 guns. She was built as an &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Indiaman" target="_blank"&gt;East Indiaman&lt;/a&gt; in about 1734 and made two voyages to India for the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company" target="_blank"&gt;East India Company&lt;/a&gt; before the Royal Navy purchased her in 1739. She formed part of a squadron under Commodore &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Anson,_1st_Baron_Anson" target="_blank"&gt;George Anson&lt;/a&gt; and was wrecked on the south coast of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile" target="_blank"&gt;Chile&lt;/a&gt; on 14 May 1741. The wreck of &lt;em&gt;Wager&lt;/em&gt; became famous for the subsequent adventures of the survivors who found themselves marooned on a &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wager_Island" target="_blank"&gt;desolate island&lt;/a&gt; in the middle of a Patagonian winter, and in particular because of the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wager_Mutiny" target="_blank"&gt;Wager Mutiny&lt;/a&gt; that followed.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>
      <enclosure url="https://www.patreon.com/api/rss/u/2bweW97Ko-8Fw3yE-z_ORgMzF4fMRqXz/e/88612902.mp3?sig=Z_aumRFCC1aDM09Ljy6c6cxc8tJXxD4r9GHm4uDjE6k%3D" length="33790472" type="audio/mpeg"/>

    <item>
      <title>Sherman's March to the Sea</title>
      <link>https://www.patreon.com/posts/shermans-march-76173169</link>
      <itunes:image href="https://c10.patreonusercontent.com/4/patreon-media/p/campaign/851711/87020c110f7e49a5a4e6b3295cabba29/eyJkIjo3MiwiaCI6MzAwMCwic3RyaXBfYWxwaGEiOjEsInciOjMwMDAsIndlYnAiOjB9/1.jpg?token-hash=L0cZ7ybBhkrdTROWz0fTtw8QpVmZVJVMUGBUowggEuk%3D"/>
      <itunes:duration>2147</itunes:duration>
      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Sherman's March to the Sea&lt;/strong&gt; (also known as the &lt;strong&gt;Savannah campaign&lt;/strong&gt; or simply &lt;strong&gt;Sherman's March&lt;/strong&gt;) was a &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_campaign" target="_blank"&gt;military campaign&lt;/a&gt; of the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War" target="_blank"&gt;American Civil War&lt;/a&gt; conducted through &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_(U.S._state)" target="_blank"&gt;Georgia&lt;/a&gt; from November 15 until December 21, 1864, by &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Tecumseh_Sherman" target="_blank"&gt;William Tecumseh Sherman&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_general_(United_States)" target="_blank"&gt;major general&lt;/a&gt; of the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Army" target="_blank"&gt;Union Army&lt;/a&gt;. The campaign began with Sherman's troops leaving the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Atlanta" target="_blank"&gt;captured&lt;/a&gt; city of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta" target="_blank"&gt;Atlanta&lt;/a&gt; on November 15 and ended with the capture of the port of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savannah,_Georgia" target="_blank"&gt;Savannah&lt;/a&gt; on December 21. His forces followed a "&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth" target="_blank"&gt;scorched earth&lt;/a&gt;" policy, destroying military targets as well as industry, infrastructure, and civilian property, disrupting the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_Confederate_States_of_America" target="_blank"&gt;Confederacy's economy and transportation networks&lt;/a&gt;. The operation debilitated the Confederacy and helped lead to its eventual surrender. Sherman's decision to operate deep within enemy territory without &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_supply_chain_management" target="_blank"&gt;supply lines&lt;/a&gt; was unusual for its time, and the campaign is regarded by some historians as an early example of modern warfare or &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_war" target="_blank"&gt;total war&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>
      <enclosure url="https://www.patreon.com/api/rss/u/2bweW97Ko-8Fw3yE-z_ORgMzF4fMRqXz/e/76173169.mp3?sig=os54VZDRmOnn73x5-GrdLDjTF-6gcb3ucMvD4dsDEvk%3D" length="34350086" type="audio/mpeg"/>
      <guid isPermaLink="false">76173169</guid>

They generally arise from &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conventional_wisdom" target="_blank"&gt;conventional wisdom&lt;/a&gt; (such as &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_wives%27_tale" target="_blank"&gt;old wives' tales&lt;/a&gt;), &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stereotypes" target="_blank"&gt;stereotypes&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superstition" target="_blank"&gt;superstitions&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fallacy" target="_blank"&gt;fallacies&lt;/a&gt;, a misunderstanding of science, or the popularization of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pseudoscience" target="_blank"&gt;pseudoscience&lt;/a&gt;. Some common misconceptions are also considered to be &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_legend" target="_blank"&gt;urban legends&lt;/a&gt;, and they are often involved in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moral_panic" target="_blank"&gt;moral panics&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;diamond hoax of 1872&lt;/strong&gt; was a swindle in which a pair of prospectors sold a false American &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond" target="_blank"&gt;diamond&lt;/a&gt; deposit to prominent businessmen in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco,_California" target="_blank"&gt;San Francisco&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City" target="_blank"&gt;New York City&lt;/a&gt;. It also triggered a brief diamond prospecting craze in the western &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_America" target="_blank"&gt;United States&lt;/a&gt;, in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arizona" target="_blank"&gt;Arizona&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mexico" target="_blank"&gt;New Mexico&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utah" target="_blank"&gt;Utah&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyoming" target="_blank"&gt;Wyoming&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado" target="_blank"&gt;Colorado&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

      <title>The Murder of John Lennon</title>
      <link>https://www.patreon.com/posts/murder-of-john-59650230</link>

      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt;On the evening of 8 December 1980, English musician &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lennon" target="_blank"&gt;John Lennon&lt;/a&gt;, formerly of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles" target="_blank"&gt;the Beatles&lt;/a&gt;, was shot and fatally wounded in the archway of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dakota" target="_blank"&gt;The Dakota&lt;/a&gt;, his residence in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City" target="_blank"&gt;New York City&lt;/a&gt;. His killer was &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_David_Chapman" target="_blank"&gt;Mark David Chapman&lt;/a&gt;, an American Beatles fan who was incensed by Lennon's lavish lifestyle and his 1966 comment that the Beatles were "&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/More_popular_than_Jesus" target="_blank"&gt;more popular than Jesus&lt;/a&gt;". Chapman said he was inspired by the fictional character &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holden_Caulfield" target="_blank"&gt;Holden Caulfield&lt;/a&gt; from &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._D._Salinger" target="_blank"&gt;J. D. Salinger&lt;/a&gt;'s novel &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Catcher_in_the_Rye" target="_blank"&gt;The Catcher in the Rye&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt;, a "phony-killer" who despises hypocrisy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

      <link>https://www.patreon.com/posts/irish-holocaust-48863222</link>
      <itunes:image href="https://c10.patreonusercontent.com/4/patreon-media/p/post/48863222/c14af30443114e27a243ca91e85fdc8a/eyJkIjo3MiwiaCI6MzAwMCwic3RyaXBfYWxwaGEiOjEsInciOjMwMDAsIndlYnAiOjB9/1.jpg?token-hash=ZO-Wcd2Inl33L5ootZkUyQklVKUyEjoa8BfgrMWz-2I%3D"/>
      <itunes:duration>2876</itunes:duration>
      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Great Famine&lt;/strong&gt; (&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_language" target="_blank"&gt;Irish&lt;/a&gt;: &lt;em&gt;an Gorta Mór&lt;/em&gt; &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Irish" target="_blank"&gt;[anˠ ˈɡɔɾˠt̪ˠə ˈmˠoːɾˠ]&lt;/a&gt;), also known as the &lt;strong&gt;Great Hunger&lt;/strong&gt;, the &lt;strong&gt;Great Starvation&lt;/strong&gt;, the &lt;strong&gt;Famine&lt;/strong&gt; (mostly within Ireland) or the &lt;strong&gt;Irish Potato Famine&lt;/strong&gt; (mostly outside Ireland),&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinealy19945-1" target="_blank"&gt;[1]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Neill20091-2" target="_blank"&gt;[2]&lt;/a&gt; was a period of mass &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starvation" target="_blank"&gt;starvation&lt;/a&gt; and disease in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland" target="_blank"&gt;Ireland&lt;/a&gt; from 1845 to 1852.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinealy1994xv-3" target="_blank"&gt;[3]&lt;/a&gt; With the most severely affected areas in the west and south of Ireland, where the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Irish_language#Nineteenth_and_twentieth_centuries" target="_blank"&gt;Irish language was dominant&lt;/a&gt;, the period was contemporaneously known in Irish as &lt;em&gt;An Drochshaol&lt;/em&gt;,&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-4" target="_blank"&gt;[4]&lt;/a&gt; loosely translated as "the hard times" (or literally "the bad life"). The worst year of the period was 1847, known as "Black '47".&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-cuiv-5" target="_blank"&gt;[5]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-6" target="_blank"&gt;[6]&lt;/a&gt; During the Great Hunger, about 1 million people died and more than a million &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_diaspora" target="_blank"&gt;fled the country&lt;/a&gt;,&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoss2002226-7" target="_blank"&gt;[7]&lt;/a&gt; causing the country's population to fall by 20%–25%, in some towns falling as much as 67% between 1841 and 1851.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinealy1994357-8" target="_blank"&gt;[8]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-9" target="_blank"&gt;[9]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-irishcentral.com-10" target="_blank"&gt;[10]&lt;/a&gt; Between 1845 and 1855, no less than 2.1 million people left Ireland, primarily on &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Packet_boat" target="_blank"&gt;packet ships&lt;/a&gt; but also &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steamboats" target="_blank"&gt;steamboats&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barque" target="_blank"&gt;barks&lt;/a&gt;—one of the greatest mass exoduses from a single island in history.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-11" target="_blank"&gt;[11]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)#cite_note-12" target="_blank"&gt;[12]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

It was located on the northeast bank of the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_River" target="_blank"&gt;James (Powhatan) River&lt;/a&gt; about 2.5 mi (4 km) southwest of the center of modern &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williamsburg,_Virginia" target="_blank"&gt;Williamsburg&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia#cite_note-2" target="_blank"&gt;[1]&lt;/a&gt; It was established by the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Company_of_London" target="_blank"&gt;Virginia Company of London&lt;/a&gt; as "James Fort" on May 4, 1607 &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" target="_blank"&gt;O.S.&lt;/a&gt; (May 14, 1607 &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" target="_blank"&gt;N.S.&lt;/a&gt;),&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia#cite_note-3" target="_blank"&gt;[2]&lt;/a&gt; and was considered permanent after a brief abandonment in 1610. It followed several failed attempts, including the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_Colony" target="_blank"&gt;Lost Colony of Roanoke&lt;/a&gt;, established in 1585 on &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_Island" target="_blank"&gt;Roanoke Island&lt;/a&gt;. Jamestown served as the colonial capital from 1616 until 1699.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

    <item>
      <title>Episode 77: The Nazino Affair</title>
      <link>https://www.patreon.com/posts/episode-77-21809848</link>
      <itunes:image href="https://c10.patreonusercontent.com/4/patreon-media/p/post/21809848/7335ba4646484294804385215d53c444/eyJkIjo3MiwiaCI6MzAwMCwic3RyaXBfYWxwaGEiOjEsInciOjMwMDAsIndlYnAiOjB9/1.png?token-hash=PuFCkLQ4zQPZEdzYFwco9yL_TOKLjhLDWn65psF0FnQ%3D"/>
      <itunes:duration>2271</itunes:duration>
      <description>&lt;html&gt;&lt;p&gt; &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Nazino affair&lt;/strong&gt; (&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language" target="_blank"&gt;Russian&lt;/a&gt;: Назинская трагедия, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Russian" target="_blank"&gt;translit.&lt;/a&gt; &lt;em&gt;Nazinskaya Tragediya&lt;/em&gt;) was the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_deportation" target="_blank"&gt;mass deportation&lt;/a&gt; of 6,000 people to Nazino Island in the &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union" target="_blank"&gt;Soviet Union&lt;/a&gt; in May 1933. The deportees, mostly &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_prisoners" target="_blank"&gt;political prisoners&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petty_criminal" target="_blank"&gt;petty criminals&lt;/a&gt;, were forcibly sent to the small, isolated island in &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Siberian_Krai" target="_blank"&gt;Western Siberia&lt;/a&gt;, located 540 kilometers (340 mi) northwest of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomsk,_Russia" target="_blank"&gt;Tomsk&lt;/a&gt;, to construct a "&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_settlements_in_the_Soviet_Union" target="_blank"&gt;special settlement&lt;/a&gt;". They were abandoned with only &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour" target="_blank"&gt;flour&lt;/a&gt; for food, and little in the way of tools, clothing, or shelter, and those who attempted to leave were killed by armed guards.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazino_affair#cite_note-1" target="_blank"&gt;[1]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazino_affair#cite_note-STimes-2" target="_blank"&gt;[2]&lt;/a&gt; The conditions of the island led to widespread &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disease" target="_blank"&gt;disease&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abuse_of_power" target="_blank"&gt;abuse of power&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violence" target="_blank"&gt;violence&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannibalism" target="_blank"&gt;cannibalism&lt;/a&gt;. Within thirteen weeks, over 4,000 of the deportees related to Nazino Island had died or disappeared, and a majority of the survivors were in ill health.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazino_affair#cite_note-3" target="_blank"&gt;[3]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazino_affair#cite_note-STimes-2" target="_blank"&gt;[2]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Nazino affair was virtually unknown until 1988, when an investigation by &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_(society)" target="_blank"&gt;Memorial&lt;/a&gt;began during the &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glasnost" target="_blank"&gt;glasnost&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt; reforms in the Soviet Union. The events were popularized in 2002 when reports of a September 1933 special commission by the Communist Party of Western Siberia were published by Memorial.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>

      <itunes:image href="https://c10.patreonusercontent.com/4/patreon-media/p/post/16716889/9787031850a4413abac39a69604ae3d8/eyJkIjo3MiwiaCI6MzAwMCwic3RyaXBfYWxwaGEiOjEsInciOjMwMDAsIndlYnAiOjB9/1.png?token-hash=EtPrCelIaxgczoMvmKsieeANf1IiMDPCkNxbgZoxXVk%3D"/>

We promise to get next week's to you extra early!&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;br/&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Charles Ponzi&lt;/strong&gt;, (born &lt;strong&gt;Carlo Pietro Giovanni Guglielmo Tebaldo Ponzi&lt;/strong&gt;; March 3, 1882 – January 18, 1949), was an &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_people" target="_blank"&gt;Italian&lt;/a&gt; swindler and &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Con_artist" target="_blank"&gt;con artist&lt;/a&gt; in the U.S. and Canada. His aliases include &lt;em&gt;Charles Ponci&lt;/em&gt;, &lt;em&gt;Carlo&lt;/em&gt;, and &lt;em&gt;Charles P. Bianchi&lt;/em&gt;.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Ponzi#cite_note-1" target="_blank"&gt;[1]&lt;/a&gt; Born and raised in Italy, he became known in the early 1920s as a swindler in North America for his money-making scheme. He promised clients a 50% profit within 45 days, or 100% profit within 90 days, by buying discounted &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postal_reply_coupons" target="_blank"&gt;postal reply coupons&lt;/a&gt; in other countries and redeeming them at face value in the United States as a form of &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitrage" target="_blank"&gt;arbitrage&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Ponzi#cite_note-time-2" target="_blank"&gt;[2]&lt;/a&gt;&lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Ponzi#cite_note-take-3" target="_blank"&gt;[3]&lt;/a&gt; In reality, Ponzi was paying earlier investors using the investments of later investors. While this type of fraudlent investment scheme was not originally invented by Ponzi, it became so identified with him that it now is referred to as a &lt;a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponzi_scheme" target="_blank"&gt;Ponzi scheme&lt;/a&gt;. His scheme ran for over a year before it collapsed, costing his "investors" $20 million. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;br/&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;br/&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/html&gt;</description>
